[{:}{*}]_{2}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{{\{j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x:=*\;]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\;\;\quad{x\notin FV(p)}

B.4.
UAPC
First-Order Axioms

The first-order axioms provide mechanisms to handle quantifiers in a proof. When applied, the axiom
\forall i
eliminates the universal quantifier from premise to conclusion by instantiating
x
with
e
. The axiom propagates the binding
\forall x
to each of the formulas
P
and
Q
. The axiom
V_{\forall}
wraps a formula that does not mention
x
in the binding
\forall x
, and the axiom
\exists
translates between universal and existential quantifiers.

M:
\forall i

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,(\forall x\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x))\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(e)

M:
\forall\to

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\forall x\;(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q)\to(\forall x\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\to\forall x\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q)

M:
V_{\forall}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\to\forall x\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\;\quad{(x\notin FV(p))}

M:
\exists

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\left(\lnot\forall x\lnot\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\right)\leftrightarrow\exists x\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P

B.5.
UAPC
Differential Equation Axioms

In this section, we give the axioms that allow us to maneuver differential equations in a proof.

M:
DW

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{\vec{k}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{j}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{k}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q](\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P)

M:
DI

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{m}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{n}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\to[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{k}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q](\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{l}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{n}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P)^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow([\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{k}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\leftrightarrow[?\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P)

M:
DC

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{i{\curlywedgeuparrow}j}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{h}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{m}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{n}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{i{\curlywedgeuparrow}j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{k}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{h}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C

\displaystyle\rightarrow([\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\leftrightarrow[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{i}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{h}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\land\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P)

M:
DG

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{m_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{j}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{m}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y^{\prime}=a(x)y+b(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P

M:
DE
1

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{\vec{j}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q][\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{m}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}:=f(x)]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{j}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)\;\;

\displaystyle\quad{(x^{\prime}\notin FV(p))}

M:
DE
2

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\{{\vec{j}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\chi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q][\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{m}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}:=f(x)]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{j}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=f(x)\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)\;\;

\displaystyle\quad{(x^{\prime}\in FV(p(x)))}

For example, the axioms M:DE
1
and M:DE
2
translate between differential equations and variable assignments. We define differential assignment in two axioms that differ depending on whether
x
is mentioned in
FV(p)
. If
x\in FV(p(x))
, M:DE
2
enforces that the only admissible mutation for the differential equation and differential assignment is the identity mutation, i.e. these should remain untouched. Alternatively, if
x\notin FV(p)
, then the postcondition must be independent of the differential equation and differential assignment, so we allow them to take on any mutation. The reasoning behind these axioms is analogous to the discussion for axioms M:
[:=]_{1}
and M:
[:=]_{2}
.

The axiom M:DW rephrases the differential equation by extracting the evolution domain constraint as an implication of the postcondition. If the evolution domain constraint is false, then the postcondition holds. No usage information can be discerned from this rule alone so the output set allows labels to take on any mutation.

The axiom M:DI allows us to reason about differential invariants which can be helpful in proving properties about systems with differential equations. The test
[?Q]P
holds if and only if the postcondition is valid after running the ODE. Similar to a loop invariant, the left-hand side of the implication asserts that the induction step can assume that the domain constraint holds. If the evolution domain constraint is initially false, then vacuously
[x^{\prime}=f(x)\&Q](P)^{\prime}
holds.

The axiom M:DC is similar to the cut rule in that it cuts a formula
C
into the evolution domain constraint and claims the result to be equivalent to the original formula provided the postcondition
C
holds after running the ODE over
Q
. Since the original postcondition
P
disappears moving from conclusion to premise in the proof, the proof calculus enforces that the labels on
\vec{m}
and
\vec{n}
remain unmutated to ensure validity.

The axiom M:DG allows us to introduce a new differential equation, or differential ghost, that facilitates the proof. The variable
y
should be new and not appear in the right-hand side or in
a(x)
or in
b(x)
.

B.6.
UAPC
Differential Axioms

This section provides the differential axioms of
UAPC
. Analogous to the
UAPC
axioms, these axioms can be used in a proof with rules M:CQR and M:CQL.

M:
DS

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{i_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{j}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},m_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\left([\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}=c()\&\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q(x)]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)\right)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\leftrightarrow\left(\forall\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\geq 0((\forall\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0\leq s\leq t\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q(x+c()s))\to[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x:=x+c()t]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x))\right)

M:
c^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(c())^{\prime}=0

M:
x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(x)^{\prime}=x^{\prime}

M:
+^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e+k)^{\prime}=(e)^{\prime}+(k)^{\prime}

M:
-^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e-k)^{\prime}=(e)^{\prime}-(k)^{\prime}

M:
\cdot^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e\cdot k)^{\prime}=(e)^{\prime}\cdot k+e\cdot(k)^{\prime}

M:
/^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{l_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(e/k)^{\prime}=((e)^{\prime}\cdot k-e\cdot(k)^{\prime})/k^{2}

M:
\circ^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\chi\uplus\{i_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},k_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y:=g(x)][\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y^{\prime}:=1]\left(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{k}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(f(g(x)))^{\prime}=(f(y))^{\prime}\cdot(g(x))^{\prime}\right)

B.7.
UAPC
Derived Rules

These rules are derivable from the core usage-aware proof calculus. In proofs, it is often more efficient to apply these rules directly, so we extend
UAPC
to accommodate analysis of these rules. The rules M:
W
R, M:
W
L, M:
W
LR are derived from the weakening rules above, and M:cutR and M:cutL are derived from M:cut. The rules M:
\leftrightarrow
cR and M:
\leftrightarrow
cL handle the symmetry of formula equivalence, and the rule M:
{\rightarrow}{2}{\leftrightarrow}
allows us to reason about equivalence in terms of implication by moving from premise to conclusion.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{W}R}}\;\;\frac{\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega\uplus\{{\vec{i}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{W}L}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega\uplus\{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{W}LR}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega\uplus\{{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}cutR}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{j}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}cutL}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{i}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{i}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\leftrightarrow$cR}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\leftrightarrow$cL}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}${\rightarrow}{2}{\leftrightarrow}$}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\chi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

B.8.
UAPC
Leaf Rules

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}id}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{{\vec{i}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}QE}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{DA(\vec{i},\vec{j}\mid\Psi)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\mathbb{R}$}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{DA(\vec{i},\vec{j}\mid\Psi)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\top$R}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{i_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{i}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{true},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}auto}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{DA(\vec{i},\vec{j}\mid\Psi)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\bot$L}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{i}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{false},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{i_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

Appendix C
Metatheory

Definition C.1 (
Uniqueness
).

Let
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{i}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{j}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
. A singleton label
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is
unique
iff
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\forall\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}},\vec{\mathbf{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\setminus\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l
.

Definition C.2 (
Freshness
).

A singleton label
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l
is
fresh
for a valid sequent
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{i}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{j}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
iff
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\notin\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Psi\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}
.

Property 4 (
Labeling schema
).

The labels
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}
in the conclusion of a proof
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{i}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{j}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
are singleton and unique. The labels of a proof tree contain either singletons that appear in the conclusion of the proof or that are fresh.

Lemma C.3 (
Well-formed sets
).

If
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{i}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{\mathbf{j}}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
is valid and the labels in
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Psi\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
are unique, then for all
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Psi
each label in
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{i}},\vec{\mathbf{j}}
appears exactly once in
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Sigma
.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward, following by structural induction on
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
. At each step, we note that label uniqueness with respect to sets is preserved by the operations on sets
\uplus
and set difference.
∎

Lemma C.4 (
Composition of mutations
).

If
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}A
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}B
are sets s.t.
\forall\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,1}A

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\notin\color[rgb]{0,0,1}B
, then
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mu_{A\cup B}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mu_{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mu_{B}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))\equiv\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mu_{B}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\mu_{A}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on the size of
A
.

Base case:
If
\left|A\right|=0
, then
A=\emptyset
and hence

\mu_{A\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{A}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))\equiv\mu_{B}(\mu_{A}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))

Inductive case:
Suppose that
\mu_{A^{\prime}\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{A^{\prime}}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
for
\left|A^{\prime}\right|=n
. We need to show that
\mu_{A\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{A}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
for
A=l_{\mathcal{M}}\cup A^{\prime}
where
l_{\mathcal{M}}\notin A^{\prime}
(i.e.
\left|A\right|=n+1
). By definition of
A
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{A}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))

\displaystyle\equiv\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}\cup A^{\prime}}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))

\displaystyle\equiv\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{A}^{\prime}(\mu_{B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)))

\displaystyle\quad{(\text{by $A\cap B=\emptyset$, i.e., $l_{\mathcal{M}}\notin B$ for all $\mathcal{M}$})}

\displaystyle\equiv\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{A^{\prime}\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))

\displaystyle\quad{(\text{by IH})}

Since
l_{\mathcal{M}}\notin A^{\prime}\;\text{for all}\;\mathcal{M}
and
A\cap B=\emptyset
, it follows that
l_{\mathcal{M}}\notin A^{\prime}\cup B\;\text{for all}\;\mathcal{M}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{A^{\prime}\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))

\displaystyle\equiv\mu_{(l_{\mathcal{M}}\cup A^{\prime})\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)

\displaystyle\equiv\mu_{A\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\;

\displaystyle\quad{(\text{by definition of $A$})}

The argument for
\mu_{A\cup B}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{B}(\mu_{A}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
follows a similar argument.

\square

∎

Lemma 2 (
Monotonicity
)..

If
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
, then
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by nested induction, first on the size of
\Omega
, and then on the depth of the proof tree.

Base case.
If
\left|\Omega\right|=0
, then it must be that
\Omega=\emptyset
. So,

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
(vacuously).
Then the desired result follows by the assumption that
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all choices of
\mu_{\Sigma}
.

Inductive step.
Assume that
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all choices of
\mu_{\Sigma}
implies

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
\left|\Omega^{\prime}\right|=n
. We need to show that

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\Omega
such that
\left|\Omega\right|=n+1
.
Fix an arbitrary
\Omega^{\prime}
, and let
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}\cup\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}
for some
l_{\mathcal{M}}
. Since
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\Omega
, the proof proceeds in two subcases (whether or not
l
appears in
\Sigma
):

Case 1:
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\Omega
,
l_{\mathcal{N}}\in\Sigma
(
\exists\;\mathcal{N}
).
Suppose that
l_{\mathcal{N}}\in\Sigma
for some
\mathcal{N}
, i.e. both
\Sigma
and
\Omega
mention label
l
. Letting
\Sigma=\Sigma^{\prime}\cup\{l_{\mathcal{N}}\}
, it follows by definition of
\uplus
that

\Sigma\uplus\Omega=\{l_{\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}}\}\cup\left(\Sigma^{\prime}\uplus\Omega^{\prime}\right)\;\quad{(\dagger)}

Since
l\notin\Omega^{\prime}
, by uniqueness of labels, we have

\left(\dagger\right)=\left(\{l_{\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}}\}\cup\Sigma^{\prime}\right)\uplus\Omega^{\prime}

Since
\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathcal{N}
and
\Sigma=\Sigma^{\prime}\cup\{l_{\mathcal{N}}\}
, it must be that any choice of
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}}\}\cup\Sigma^{\prime}}
is already included in the choices for
\mu_{\Sigma}
. Therefore, since
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
, it follows that
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}}\}\cup\Sigma^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}\sqcap\mathcal{N}}\}}\cup\Sigma^{\prime}
.
Then the desired result follows by the induction hypothesis.

Case 2:
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\Omega
,
l_{\mathcal{N}}\notin\Sigma
(for all
\mathcal{N}
).
Suppose that
l_{\mathcal{N}}\notin\Sigma
, i.e.
\Sigma
has no constraints on
l
. Then by definition of
\uplus
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma\uplus\Omega

\displaystyle=\Sigma\uplus\left(\Omega^{\prime}\cup\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\cup(\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime})

We need to show that
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\cup(\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime})}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid. To this end, we need to show that
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}}(\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
is valid.
The proof proceeds in two subcases (whether or not
l_{\mathcal{M}}
labels an atom in
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
):

Subcase 1:
l_{\mathcal{M}}
does not label any atoms in
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
.
Then, by definition of
\mu
:

\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\cup(\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime})}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)

By IH,
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid, and we conclude that
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\cup(\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime})}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid.

Subcase 2:
l_{\mathcal{M}}
labels some atom in
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
.
The proof proceeds by an inner induction on the depth of the proof tree. Writing
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
as
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
, we have the following:

Base case.
If the depth of the proof tree is
1
, then it must be a leaf rule, i.e. M:id, M:auto, M:
\mathbb{R}
, M:
\top R
, M:
\bot L
.

Case M:id
Suppose that
l_{\mathcal{M}}
was generated by M:id, and let
\Gamma^{\prime}=P,\Gamma_{0}
and
\Delta^{\prime}=P,\Delta_{0}
.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}id}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{0}\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{{\vec{i}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{h}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{h}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta_{0}}

Let
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\Psi
be an arbitrary label of some atom in
P,\Gamma_{0}\vdash P,\Delta_{0}
(i.e.
\vec{i}
,
\vec{j}
,
\vec{k}
,
\vec{h}
). Observe that this case holds vacuously because in this case
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\Psi
is a label of some atom in
P,\Gamma_{0}\vdash P,\Delta_{0}
and
l_{\mathcal{N}}\notin\{{\vec{i}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{h}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi
for any
\mathcal{N}
.

Cases M:auto, M:
\mathbb{R}
, M:
\top R
, M:
\bot L
.
The proofs of these cases each follow arguments analogous to the one provided for M:id. The proof cases for M:auto, M:
\mathbb{R}
rely on definition
4.2
which enforces that the set
DA(\vec{i},\vec{j})
mentions every label in
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
.

Inductive step.
Suppose that there exists a proof of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime\prime}
with depth
n
for some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}
. We need to show that there exists a proof of
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}}(\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime})
with depth
n+1
. By observation
2
, all other labels in
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
already appear in
\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime}
. So there are only a select number of rules that could have generated
l_{\mathcal{M}}
, i.e. for which
l_{\mathcal{M}}
appears in the output set of the conclusion but not of the premise. In other words, the only cases we need to consider here are the following:

Case M:CTR:
Suppose that
l_{\mathcal{M}}
was generated by M:CTR.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}CTR}}\;\;\frac{\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e=k}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega\uplus\{{\vec{i}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c(e)=c(k),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

Here, we need to show that there exists a proof of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash c(e)=c(k),\Delta))
for
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\{{\vec{i}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}
.
Observe that in all cases,
l_{\mathcal{M}}
does not label any atoms in
\vdash e=k
. Therefore, it follows by definition of
\mu
that for all choices of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}
:

\left(\vdash e=k\right)\equiv\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\vdash e=k)

By the induction hypothesis,
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash c(e)=c(k),\Delta)
is valid. Since
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash c(e)=c(k),\Delta)
is valid, there exists a proof of it, and hence there exists a proof of
\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash e=k)
, i.e.
\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash e=k)
is valid. Since
l_{\mathcal{M}}
does not appear in
\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e=k
, there exists a proof of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash e=k))
. Then by
dL
rule CTR, there exists a proof of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash c(e)=c(k),\Delta))
.

Case M:CTL:
This case follows in analogy to rule M:CTR.

Case M:
W
R:
Suppose that
l_{\mathcal{M}}
was generated by M:
W
R.

\quad{\text{M{:}{W}R}}\;\;\frac{\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega\uplus\{{\vec{i}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

We need to show that there exists a proof of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta))
for
l_{\mathcal{M}}\in\{{\vec{i}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}
. By the induction hypothesis,
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)
is valid, and hence there exists a proof of it. Then there exists a proof of the premise
\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash P)
, i.e.
\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash P)
is valid.
Since
\vec{i},\vec{k}
do not appear in
\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P
, we have that
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\vdash P))
is valid for any choice of
l_{\mathcal{M}}
. Therefore, by application of the
dL
rule
W
R,
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta))
is valid.

Cases M:
W
L, M:
W
LR, M:WR, M:WL, M:GVR:
These cases each follow arguments analogous to the one given for M:
W
R.

In all cases, there exists a proof of
\mu_{l_{\mathcal{M}}}(\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta))
. Therefore, it must be valid. So it follows by lemma
3.6
that
\mu_{\{l_{\mathcal{M}}\}\cup(\Sigma\uplus\Omega^{\prime})}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\Omega^{\prime}
. In general, we have shown that
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\Omega
.

\square

∎

Corollary 1 (
Monotonicity with freshness
)..

If
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid, then
\mu_{\Sigma\setminus{\vec{l}}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid where
\vec{l}
fresh for
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
.

Proof.

Since
\vec{l}
is fresh, it follows by observation
1
that
\vec{l}
must not label any atom in
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
. Therefore, it follows by definition of
\mu
that:

\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\equiv\mu_{\Sigma\setminus{\vec{l}}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)

Since
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid by assumption, it follows that
\mu_{\Sigma\setminus{\vec{l}}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid.

\square

∎

Theorem 1 (
Soundness of
UAPC
)..

If
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, then for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
,
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by structural induction over the usage-aware proof calculus.

Case M:
\land
R.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is M:
\land
R:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\land$R}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q
with the last rule M:
\land
R, there must exist (smaller) proofs of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
. From the induction hypothesis applied to the proofs of these premises, we have:

(1)

\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Sigma}

(2)

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash Q,\Delta)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Omega}

Since
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)
is valid, and by lemma
5.2
applied to (1), we have

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)\text{ is valid for a fixed arbitrary $\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}$.}\;\;(3)

Similarly, since
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash Q,\Delta)
is valid, it follows by lemma
5.2
applied to (2) that

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash Q,\Delta)\text{ is valid for the same $\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}$. }\;\;(4)

By the
dL

\land
R proof rule applied to (3) and (4), we have

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash P\land Q,\Delta)
is valid.

Since our choice of
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
was arbitrary, the desired result holds for
any

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
.

Cases M:
\lor
L, M:
\leftrightarrow
R, M:
\to
L, M:
\leftrightarrow
L.

The proof cases for these rules each follow an argument analogous to the one provided for M:
\land
R.

Case M:cut.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is M:cut:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}cut}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega)\setminus{\vec{l}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{(\forall C_{a}\in\text{atoms}(C).\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\phi(C_{a},(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})})}

Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega)\setminus{\vec{l}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, there must exist (smaller) proofs of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C
.
So, by the induction hypothesis applied to the proofs of these premises, we have:

•

\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Sigma}
.

•

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Omega}
.

Now, we want to show that
\mu_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega)\setminus{\vec{l}}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid. Since
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)
is valid, it follows by lemma
5.2
that for a fixed arbitrary
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
:

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

From
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)
, it follows by lemma
5.2
that (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
):

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)\;\text{ is valid}.

Then, by the
dL
proof rule cut (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
):

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

Since
\vec{l}
is fresh for
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
, it follows by lemma
5.3
that:

\mu_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega)\setminus{\vec{l}}}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

Since
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
was arbitrary, the desired result holds for
any

\mu_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega)\setminus{\vec{l}}}
.

Cases M:MR, M:ML.

The proof case for this rule follows an argument analogous to the one provided for M:cut.

Case M:cutR.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is M:cutR:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}cutR}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{($\vec{j}$ fresh)}}

This case is similar to M:cut. Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, there must exist (smaller) proofs of

(1)

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
and

(2)

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\to\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
.

By applying the induction hypothesis to the proof of
(1)
, we have

\mu_{\Omega}({\Gamma}\vdash{Q},{\Delta})\;\;\text{is valid, for any $\mu_{\Omega}$.}

By applying the induction hypothesis to the proof of
(2)
, we have

\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash{Q}\to{P},\Delta)\;\;{\text{is valid, for any $\mu_{\Sigma}$.}}

Now, we want to show that
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)
is valid. Since
\mu_{\Omega}({\Gamma}\vdash{Q},{\Delta})
is valid, it follows by lemma
5.2
that for a fixed arbitrary
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
:

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}({\Gamma}\vdash{Q},{\Delta})\;\text{ is valid}.

From
\mu_{\Sigma}({\Gamma}\vdash{Q}\to{P},\Delta)
, it follows by lemma
5.2
that (for the same
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
):

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}({\Gamma}\vdash{Q}\to{P},\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

Then, by the
dL
proof rule cutR (for the same
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
):

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

Since
\vec{j}
is fresh for
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
, it follows by lemma
5.3
that:

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}(\Gamma\vdash P,\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

Since
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
was arbitrary, the desired result holds for
any

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{j}}}
.

Case M:cutL.

The proof case for this rule follows an argument analogous to the one provided for M:cutR.

Case M:CER.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is M:CER:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}CER}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus{\vec{l}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{(\forall Q_{a}\in\text{atoms}(Q).\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\phi(Q_{a},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})})}

Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\vec{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{\vec{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q_{0}),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, there must exist (smaller) proofs of

(1)

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{m}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{j}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, and

(2)

\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P

By the induction hypothesis applied to (1), we have that for all
\mu_{\Omega}
:

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash C(P_{0}),\Delta)\;\text{is valid}.

Likewise, applying the induction hypothesis to (2) yields that for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
:

\mu_{\Sigma}(\vdash P_{0}\leftrightarrow Q_{0})\;\text{is valid}

All together we have

•

\mu_{\Sigma}(\vdash P_{0}\leftrightarrow Q_{0})
is valid for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
.

•

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash C(P_{0}),\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Omega}
.

By application of lemma
5.2
, we have for all
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}

•

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}(\vdash P_{0}\leftrightarrow Q_{0})
is valid

•

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash C(P_{0}),\Delta)
is valid

Fix an arbitrary
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
. Then by the
dL
CER rule, we have:

\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash C(Q_{0}),\Delta)\text{ is valid}.

By freshness of
\vec{j}
, it follows by lemma
5.3
that

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\vec{j}}(\Gamma\vdash C(Q_{0}),\Delta)\text{ is valid}.

Since
\mu_{\Omega\uplus\Sigma}
was arbitrary, the result holds for any
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\vec{j}}
.

Case M:CEL.

The proof case for this rule follows an argument analogous to the one provided for M:CER.

Case M:CQR.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is M:CQR:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}CQR}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(k),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k=e}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\{l\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(e),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{\text{(${l}$ fresh)}}

Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\{l\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(e),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, there must exist (smaller) proofs of

(1)

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(k),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, and

(2)

\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k=e

The proof proceeds by an inner induction on the structure of
\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{{l}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k=e
:

By application of the induction hypothesis, it follows that for any
\mu_{\Sigma}
,
\mu_{\Sigma}(k=e)
is valid.
By the induction hypothesis applied to (1), we have that for all
\mu_{\Omega}
:

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash p(k),\Delta).

All together we have

•

\mu_{\Sigma}(\vdash k=e)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
.

•

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash p(k),\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\Omega}
.

By application of lemma
5.2
, it follows that

•

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}(\vdash k=e)
is valid for all
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}
.

•

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}(\Gamma\vdash p(k),\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}
.

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}
. Then by the
dL
CQR rule, we have:

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}(\Gamma\vdash p(e),\Delta)\text{ is valid}.

By the freshness of
l
, it follows by application of lemma
5.3
that

\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\{l\}}(\Gamma\vdash p(e),\Delta)\text{ is valid}.

Since
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)}
was arbitrary, the result holds for any
\mu_{(\Omega\uplus\Sigma)\setminus\{l\}}
.

•

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J

is valid for any
\mu_{\Sigma}

\mu_{\Omega}

\mu_{\Theta}

\mu_{\Sigma}
,
\mu_{\Omega}

that (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega\uplus\Theta}
):

dL
loop proof rule (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega\uplus\Theta}

\vec{l}
and from lemma
5.3
, we have:

\mu_{(\Sigma\uplus\Omega\uplus\Theta)\setminus\vec{l}}(\Gamma\vdash[\alpha^{*}]P,\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

was arbitrary, the desired result holds for

Theorem 2 (
Completeness of
UAPC
)..

If
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is valid, then for all
\Psi
there exists
\Sigma
such that
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is valid, it has a proof in the
dL
proof calculus. The argument for completeness then follows by structural induction on the proof of
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
. In each case, we observe that there is a matching (or set of matching) proof rules in the usage-aware proof calculus that each generate an output set
\Sigma
.

Case
\land
R

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is
\land
R:

\quad{\text{$\land$R}}\;\;\frac{{\Gamma}\vdash{P}\quad{\Gamma}\vdash{Q}}{{\Gamma}\vdash{P}\land{Q}}

Let
\Psi
be an arbitrary input set. Then by the induction hypothesis, there exist output sets
\Sigma
and
\Omega
such that
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q
, respectively. Therefore, it follows by the rule M:
\land
R that
\Sigma\uplus\Omega
is an output set for
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q
.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\land$R}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}

Case
[:=]

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is
[:=]
:

\quad{[:=]}\;\;\vdash[{x:=e}]{p(x)}\leftrightarrow{p(e)}\\

Let
\Psi
be an arbitrary input set. The proof proceeds in two subcases, where
x
appears in the postcondition (i.e.
x\in FV(p(x))
) and where
x
does not appear in the postcondition (i.e.
x\notin FV(p)
).

Subcase
x\in FV(p(x))

If
x\in FV(p(x))
, then it follows by M:
[:=]_{1}
that
\{j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi
is an output set for
\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x:=e]\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(e)
.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$[:=]_{1}$}}\;\;\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}id\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x:=e]\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(x)\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p(e)\;\;\quad{x\in FV(p(x))}

Subcase

, then it follows by M:

that

is an output set for

.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$[:=]_{2}$}}\;\;\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{{\{j_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},\vec{k}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{j}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x:=e]\;\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p\;\;\quad{x\notin FV(p)}

, DE

.

Case id

\quad{\text{id}}\;\;\frac{}{{P},{\Gamma}\vdash{P},{\Delta}}

Let
\Psi
be an arbitrary input set. Therefore, it follows by the rule M:id that
\{{\vec{i}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi
is an output set for

.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}id}}\;\;\frac{}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\{{\vec{i}{\curlywedgeuparrow}\vec{j}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{k}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}},{\vec{l}}_{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}R\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\}\uplus\Psi}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{l}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}

Case dW

We assume that all
dL

proof calculus. Since the rule dW is derivable from the differential equation axiom DW, the completeness for this case follows from the cases given above.

The proof follows an argument analogous to the one presented for dW.

D.5.
UAPC
+
Axioms, Differential Axioms, and First-order Axioms

The axioms are identical to those of
UAPC
since these are essentially leaf rules.

D.6.
Metatheory

Theorem D.1 (
Soundness of
UAPC
+
).

If
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, then for all
\mu_{\Sigma}
,
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid.

Proof sketch.

The argument follows by lemma
5.2
and theorem
5.1
.
\square

Theorem D.2 (
Completeness of
UAPC
+
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is valid, then for all
\Psi
there exists
\Sigma
such that
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
.

Proof sketch.

The argument follows by lemma
5.2
and theorem
5.4
.
\square

D.7.
Extension of
UAPC
to Proof Diagnostics

In this section, we extend the
UAPC
proof calculus with the capability of tracking cut diagnostics. We accomplish this by retaining the fresh labels of cut atoms which we would normally discard in
UAPC
. We list only the rules that change (which are the ones that introduce fresh labels), and note that all other rules remain the same.

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M${}_{\texttt{d}}$:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}loop}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{n}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega\uplus\Theta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,[\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{m}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha^{*}]\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{(\forall D.7.1.
Metatheory

The soundness and completeness theorems follow from
UAPC
, with slight modifications to the argument in the cases noted above.

Theorem D.3 (
Soundness
).

If
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
is valid, then
\mu_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Sigma}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid for all
\mu_{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Sigma}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one for
UAPC
. We sketch an example case, the canonical one being
M
{}_{\texttt{d}}
:
cut:

Case
M
{}_{\texttt{d}}
:
cut.

Suppose the last rule in the proof tree is
M
{}_{\texttt{d}}
:
cut:

\quad{\text{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}M${}_{\texttt{d}}$:\color[rgb]{0,0,0}cut}^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\;\;\frac{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta\quad\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}\quad{(\forall C_{a}\in\text{atoms}(C).\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\phi(C_{a},(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta),\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})})}

Since there exists a proof of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
, there must exist (smaller) proofs of
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Omega}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{k}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C
.
So, by the induction hypothesis applied to the proofs of these premises, we have:

•

\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Sigma}
.

•

\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)
is valid, for any
\mu_{\Omega}
.

Now, we want to show that
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)
is valid. Since
\mu_{\Sigma}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)
is valid, it follows by lemma
5.2
that for a fixed arbitrary
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
:

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma,C\vdash\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

From
\mu_{\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)
, it follows by lemma
5.2
that (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
):

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta,C)\;\text{ is valid}.

Then, by the
dL
proof rule cut (for the same
\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}
):

\mu_{\Sigma\uplus\Omega}(\Gamma\vdash\Delta)\;\text{ is valid}.

This is the desired result.

∎

Theorem D.4 (
Completeness
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\Delta
is valid, then for all
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Psi
there exists a
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Sigma
such that
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{i}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma\vdash\mkern-5.0mu{}^{\Psi}_{\Sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{\mathbf{{j}}}{:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta
is valid.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one for
UAPC
.
∎

D.7.2.
Diagnostics for the Parachute Example

The need for such diagnostics arises in the parachute example in Fig.
2
. By inspecting the branch (3a) output
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Theta
, any mutation (including removal) is admissible for
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}u_{1}
. Thus,
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}u_{1}
, which was freshly cut into the proof via
M
{}_{\texttt{d}}
:
loop, was unnecessary in this proof branch. In fact, this is the case overall if we were to look at the output sets of all three branches (1), (2), (3). In this extension,
\Xi
indicates that the atom labeled by
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}u_{1}
was an unnecessary cut in the proof. Soundness guarantees that the removal of this atom maintains the proof structure.
